# ARBORA-OPS requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `ARBORA-OPS-R001` | **Native MCP/REST facade with A2A unary operations then streaming.** Arbora provides a native MCP and REST host. It fronts the engine and meristem services. It first exposes unary A2A operations: card, send, get, list, and cancel. It then adds durable streaming to those same handlers. Caller chain: `arbora/a2a/mcp.py:46::register_a2a_tools` mounts `POST /a2a` and the agent card route (`arbora/mcp_server/runtime.py:930`) -> `arbora/a2a/application.py:477::create_a2a_handlers` (`json_rpc`) -> `arbora/a2a/service.py:32::A2AService` (`send_message`, `get_task`, `list_tasks`, `cancel_task`) -> `arbora/a2a/authority.py:166::WorkItemA2AAuthority`. Shape: `arbora/a2a/application.py` (JSON-RPC handlers, `_unary_request`), `arbora/a2a/service.py` and `arbora/a2a/authority.py` implement the unary card/send/get/list/cancel methods; `tasks/resubscribe` and streaming are registered but refuse until a durable event cursor exists (`_resubscribe_stub`), so streaming is the follow-on slice on the same handlers. | Integration tests exercise the A2A unary endpoints against a fixture engine, followed by a separate test proving durable streaming delivery. Test: `tests/a2a/test_application.py::test_json_rpc_unary_methods_use_typed_shared_contract`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R002` | **Scoped A2A task routing and fleet tool exposure.** Arbora routes inbound A2A tasks to the correct operation handler and exposes, through its assembled multiplexer, only the caller-authorized subset of fleet tools, including typed work-item retrieval and listing bound by control leases and read classifications. Caller chain: `arbora/a2a/mcp.py:46::register_a2a_tools` -> `arbora/a2a/application.py:477::create_a2a_handlers` -> `arbora/a2a/service.py:32::A2AService.send_message` -> `arbora/a2a/routing.py:44::OrchestratorA2ARouter.route`; fleet tool discovery: `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:317::dispatch_verb` (find) -> `arbora/mcp_server/fleet_composition.py:112::LateFleetGateway.fleet_search`. Shape: `OrchestratorA2ARouter` in `arbora/a2a/routing.py` picks the named or default agent from message metadata; `fleet_search` returns nothing without `mcp:discover` and only the caller's authorized tools; typed work-item list/get are `work.items.list` and `work.items.get` in `arbora/api/ops/work.py:94` (scope `work:read`). | Integration tests assert inbound A2A tasks resolve to the correct handler and that multiplexer tool listings are filtered per caller. Test: `tests/a2a/test_authority.py::test_router_routes_to_the_named_or_the_default_agent`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R003` | **Remove multiplexer skill/prompt harvest path.** Arbora removes the multiplexer's legacy skill and prompt harvesting and static hydration code path. Arbora sources skills and prompts only through the fleet discovery surface. Caller chain: Removal target is the harvest path in `arbora/fleet/multiplexer.py` (`_harvest_resource_bodies`, `_bounded_skill_catalog`, `_bounded_prompt_catalog`), reached from the multiplexer probe (`probe_server`); it is still present, so the removal is not yet done. Shape: Delete the harvest functions and the static skill/prompt hydration in `arbora/fleet/multiplexer.py`, and source skills and prompts only through `arbora/fleet/catalog_reader.py` and `arbora/fleet/local_skill_catalog.py`; `tests/fleet/test_harvest_inventory.py` pins the current call sites and must shrink to zero. | A regression test confirms the retired harvest module is absent and unimportable at runtime. Planned test: `tests/fleet/test_harvest_removed.py::test_multiplexer_has_no_skill_prompt_harvest_path`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R004` | **FastMCP 4 bridge for the MCP server.** The Arbora MCP bridge declares and runs on FastMCP 4. It provides native WebMCP support and a single consolidated event loop. Its own probes already exercise this version. Caller chain: `pyproject.toml:67` declares `fastmcp>=4.1.0`; startup guard `arbora/mcp_server/server.py:263` calls `_preflight_mcp_sdk_floor` (`:80`) -> `arbora/fleet/protocol_compat.py:426::check_mcp_sdk_floor`; release check `arbora/deployment/release_canary.py:67::_served_fastmcp_matches_declared` via the `arbora-release-canary` script. Shape: The runtime floor lives in `pyproject.toml`; `check_mcp_sdk_floor` refuses start on a mismatched installed pair (`MCP_SDK_FLOOR_ENFORCE`), and `release_canary.py` compares the declared FastMCP major with the installed one and reports only aggregate booleans. | A semantic test suite plus a live probe confirm the deployed bridge reports FastMCP 4. Test: `tests/deployment/test_release_canary.py::test_served_fastmcp_matches_declared_true_on_matching_major`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R005` | **Stable auth error codes from the engine contract.** Arbora consumes the engine's packaged error-code contract. This includes the stable AUTH_TENANT_MISMATCH, AUTH_AUDIENCE_MISMATCH, and AUTH_POLICY_VERSION_MISMATCH codes. Arbora refuses to start if the installed contract is missing or inconsistent with its generated bindings. Caller chain: `arbora/api/errors.py:490::to_envelope` -> `arbora/api/errors.py:112::_engine_error` -> `arbora/api/engine_errors.py::ENGINE_ERRORS` (holds `AUTH_TENANT_MISMATCH`, `AUTH_AUDIENCE_MISMATCH`, `AUTH_POLICY_VERSION_MISMATCH`); contract load at startup: `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py:95` -> `arbora/api/registry/eg_binding.py:370::load_eg_bindings`. Shape: `ENGINE_ERRORS` in `arbora/api/engine_errors.py` maps each engine code to (HTTP status, retryable); an unknown code raises `EgContractError` (`eg_binding.py:36`) so `to_envelope` fails closed, and `load_eg_bindings` raises the same error when the packaged contract is unreadable. | A contract test fails closed when the packaged engine error contract is absent or mismatched against the generated bindings. Test: `tests/api/test_errors.py::test_generated_engine_table_controls_envelope`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R006` | **Consume the engine's packaged wire-callable contract.** Arbora reads the installed engine package's contract files for methods, errors, scopes, and JSON schemas. It binds operations only for engine methods marked wire-callable in that contract. Caller chain: `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py:95` -> `arbora/api/registry/eg_binding.py:370::load_eg_bindings` (reads `methods.json`, scopes and schemas from the installed engine package) -> one generated op per wire-callable method. Shape: `load_eg_bindings` in `arbora/api/registry/eg_binding.py` builds ops only for rows with `is_wire_callable`, checks exact coverage with `_check_coverage` and `arbora/api/registry/eg_exclusions.yaml`, and takes scope and schema refs from the contract files. | A package-contract test confirms the generated registry excludes any method not marked wire-callable in the installed engine contract. Test: `tests/api/test_eg_binding.py::test_method_not_marked_wire_callable_is_not_bound`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R007` | **Immutable operation registry with digest and authorization.** Arbora defines one immutable OpSpec registry with typed enums for verb, surface, effect, and scope, producing a canonical form and a stable SHA-256 registry digest, and evaluates per-call authorization at find time. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_registry.py cover canonical-form stability, digest reproducibility, and authorized-evaluation outcomes. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R007.1` | **REST request authority.** Each `/api/v1` operation request runs under the verified session and actor that its own bearer resolved to. The REST surface binds that authority around the invocation chokepoint, as the MCP transport does per tool call. It never uses a process session or a synthesized one. An unauthenticated request answers 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. Proving test: `tests/api/test_http_session_scope.py`. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` with the bearer's session bound by `arbora/api/http/routes.py:19::caller_session_scope` around the call; an unauthenticated request is refused in `routes.py:171::endpoint` as `UNAUTHENTICATED`. Shape: `caller_session_scope` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` enters `use_actor` and `use_session` from `arbora/security/actor_context.py` and `arbora/api/contracts/session.py` for the verified caller; the 401 and `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` come from the shared error envelope. Test: `tests/api/test_http_session_scope.py::test_rest_invoke_runs_under_the_callers_verified_session`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R008` | **RETIRED: superseded by ARBORA-FLEET-R012.** `ARBORA-FLEET-R012` owns the generated engine-bound operations and their exclusions. | Not applicable. The bound evidence is on `ARBORA-FLEET-R012`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009` | **Single invoke pipeline for authority, effect and audit.** Stage: every served operation. Caller: MCP (`arbora/api/mcp/serving.py`), HTTP (`arbora/api/http/routes.py`), A2A and `fleet.call` → `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`. No other path reaches a handler. Order inside `invoke`, with the refusal code of each step: (1) registry lookup, `UNKNOWN_OP`. (2) `_static_refusal`: surface check `SURFACE_NOT_ALLOWED`, effect authority `UNAVAILABLE`. (3) `_arguments` through `validate_params` in `steps.py`, `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. (4) `_resolve` and `_bind_caller`: `authenticate` `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `principal_rule` `PRINCIPAL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `require_scopes` `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. (5) policy decision, `POLICY_DENIED` or `POLICY_UNAVAILABLE`. (6) subject read for service-executed ops, `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. (7) `_confirmation_gate`: a destructive effect without `plan_ref` returns a preview. A plan bound to another operation, parameters, actor or policy revision returns `PLAN_MISMATCH`. A used or stale plan returns `PLAN_STALE`. A plan older than `PLAN_TTL_MS` (600000 ms, `Fixed`) returns `PLAN_EXPIRED`. (8) Admin and human-approval effects need a console session, a human principal and multi-factor authentication within `MFA_FRESH_SECONDS` (900 s, `Fixed`). Otherwise `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`. (9) audit reservation before the handler; a failed reservation stops the call. An outcome-link failure returns `INDETERMINATE` and keeps the reservation (`ARBORA-OPS-R036`). Service-bound operations run through `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py`. Writes: the audit reservation and the effect journal. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R036`, `ARBORA-A2A-R007`. | Test: `tests/api/test_invoke_served.py::test_allow_deny_plan_expiry_and_step_up` (L3) drives HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It asserts one code per step above and identical results on both transports. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R010` | **Closed error enum and one response envelope.** Arbora defines a closed error-code enum, including UNAUTHENTICATED, UNKNOWN_OP, NOT_FOUND, SCOPE_REQUIRED, and POLICY_DENIED, with a generated engine-error mapping. It converts every surface's response through one shared to_envelope function. Caller chain: Every surface renders failures through `arbora/api/errors.py:490::to_envelope`: HTTP `arbora/api/http/app.py:33::_outcome_response`, MCP `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:47::_envelope`, A2A `arbora/a2a/application.py:300::_application_error` (status via `errors.py:472::a2a_error_status`). Shape: `ArboraErrorCode` (`arbora/api/errors.py:16`) is the closed enum; `ENGINE_ERRORS` in `arbora/api/engine_errors.py` maps engine codes through `_engine_error`; `to_envelope` returns (status, envelope) and needs op, request id and registry digest. | Unit tests check envelope shape and error-code mapping, and a generator diff check compares output against the packaged engine contract. Test: `tests/api/test_errors.py::test_every_arbora_code_has_one_envelope`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R011` | **Six resident MCP verbs with typed discovery.** Arbora implements the six resident MCP verbs (find, ask, why, write, act, manage) through a shared verb constructor with typed input schemas, exposes registry entries as arbora:// resources, and includes the registry digest in the server's published instructions. Caller chain: `arbora/mcp_server/runtime.py:964::_register_served_intent_surface` -> `arbora/api/mcp/serving.py:29::register_intent_surface` -> `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:370::make_verb` (six verbs) -> `verbs.py:317::dispatch_verb` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke`. Shape: `make_verb` and `native_verb` in `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py` share one `PARAMETERS` schema across `VERBS`; `arbora/api/mcp/resources.py:41::register_resources` exposes `arbora://registry` and `arbora://ops/{op_id}` filtered by caller; `verbs.py:450::mcp_instructions` embeds `registry_digest`. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_mcp_verbs.py cover verb schema validation, resource listing, and digest presence. Test: `tests/api/test_mcp_verbs.py::test_six_tools_share_one_schema_and_digest_in_instructions`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R012` | **Bounded resolver for natural-language operation lookup.** Arbora resolves natural-language and partial queries to registry operations using combined lexical and semantic ranking over generated descriptors, a tenant- and policy-partitioned outcome moving average, and a bounded result cache, falling any unmatched ask call back to a read-only natural-language query preview rather than a direct mutation. Caller chain: `arbora/mcp_server/runtime.py:980` builds `IntentResolver()` -> `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:182::_resolve_op` -> `arbora/api/mcp/resolve.py:199::IntentResolver.resolve` -> `resolve.py:441::_ask_fallback`. Shape: `IntentResolver` in `arbora/api/mcp/resolve.py` ranks `Descriptor`s lexically and semantically (`rank`, `_score`), keeps an `OrderedDict` cache bounded by `_CACHE_LIMIT` (256, `_evict_oldest`), partitions outcome feedback by `scope_ref` (tenant and policy revision), and `_ask_fallback` routes an unmatched ask to the read-only `query.nl` preview. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_resolve.py cover ranking order, cache bounds, and the preview-only fallback behavior. Test: `tests/api/test_resolve.py::test_unmatched_ask_falls_back_to_read_only_nl_preview`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R013` | **Generated HTTP API for every registry operation.** Arbora exposes each registry operation at POST /api/v1/ops/{op_id}. It also exposes generated resource routes on a dedicated sub-application. The sub-application has an authentication dependency and CSRF protection for cookie sessions. It also has cursor pagination, idempotency-key handling, and discovery endpoints for the registry and OpenAPI document. Caller chain: `arbora/mcp_server/hosted_invocation.py:60::HostedInvocation.http_application` and `arbora/mcp_server/composition.py:268` -> `arbora/api/http/app.py:58::create_api_application` -> `/ops/{op_id}` routes (`app.py:207`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `pipeline.py:1021::invoke`. Shape: `create_api_application` in `arbora/api/http/app.py` builds an isolated FastAPI sub-app with `/registry`, `/ops/{op_id}`, `/openapi.json` and `/docs` discovery routes plus generated resource routes from `op.http`; authentication is `arbora/api/http/auth.py`, cursor params merge in `routes.py:82::_merge_resource_params`, and the `Idempotency-Key` header is forwarded by `make_endpoint`. | Integration tests in tests/api/test_http_*.py cover routing, authentication, CSRF enforcement, and pagination and idempotency behavior. Test: `tests/api/test_http_projection.py::test_v1_subapp_mount_has_one_prefix`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R014` | **RETIRED: identity operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R018` owns the identity operations and `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R025` owns their registration. Frozen child `R014.1` keeps its ID. | Retired; see ARBORA-IDENTITY-R018. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R015` | **RETIRED: access operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R019` owns the access operations. The operations already exist in `arbora/api/ops/access.py`. | Retired; see ARBORA-IDENTITY-R019. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R016` | **RETIRED: capacity operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-CAPACITY-R002` owns `capacity.throttle.status` and `capacity.throttle.set_mode` in `arbora/api/ops/capacity.py`. | Retired; see ARBORA-CAPACITY-R002. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017` | **Finance and markets operations with tenant isolation (rollup).** Stage: served operations `markets.*`, `events.*`, `orders.*` and the position and paper-trading actions. Caller: MCP or HTTP → `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/finance.py` handlers → `arbora/finance/markets.py::get_market_read` (provider port) and the medulla `Finance*` methods. Ownership split: Arbora serves the operations and holds approvals. Medulla computes every figure natively (`MEDULLA-FINANCE-PRIMITIVES`). `SCION-FINANCE-SOURCES` defines the source contract. `emerald-exchange` holds only venue calls and order routing. Reads: `ARBORA-OPS-R017.1` and `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003`. Confirmed order and position changes: `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`. Subject ownership: `ARBORA-OPS-R017.2`. Writes: none by the rollup. Depends on: R017.1, R017.2, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003`, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_finance_served.py::test_two_tenants_never_cross` (L3) reads as two tenants over HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. No cross-tenant data may appear. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017.1` | **Tenant-scoped market reads under finance:read.** Arbora executes market reads server-side through the service executor under the finance:read scope, refusing with zero provider calls on a missing or mismatched tenant/account binding. Caller chain: Planned: `arbora/api/ops/finance.py` (not yet registered) via `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `arbora/finance/markets.py:90::get_market_read`. Shape: `get_market_read`, `MarketReadRequest`, `AccountBinding` and the `MarketProvider` protocol in `arbora/finance/markets.py` already refuse with `UNAVAILABLE` before any provider call on a missing or mismatched binding; scope `FinanceScope.READ` is in `arbora/api/registry/scopes.py`; the served op wrapper is the remaining slice. | Tests confirm a matching binding reaches the provider and a missing or cross-tenant binding refuses before any provider call. Test: `tests/finance/test_markets.py::test_matched_binding_dispatches_through_the_provider`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017.2` | **Position and paper-trading actions with subject ownership checks.** Stage: served write operations `positions.*` and `orders.paper_submit`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py::prepare_executor` → `runtime.check_subject_access(caller, subject)` → handler in `arbora/api/ops/finance.py`. Decision: the subject check already exists in the pipeline. The earlier block on a later engine contract is void. Rule: each operation declares `executor=Executor.SERVICE`, `principals=PrincipalRule.HUMAN_UNDELEGATED`, `subject=SubjectRef(source=SubjectSource.PARAM, path='params.account_id')`, scopes `finance:read` plus `finance:paper-trade` and `executor_scopes` `finance:paper-trade`. `prepare_executor` resolves the account id and asks `check_subject_access`. A caller who does not own the account gets `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. A request that carries an owner field gets `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`FORBIDDEN_OWNER`). Arbora calls the provider only after the check passes. A failed check makes zero provider calls and writes no audit effect. Config: none. Writes: the paper order through the venue tool and the audit reservation. Depends on: `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003.5`, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py:104::prepare_executor` -> `check_subject_access` (`executor.py:117`) -> planned handler in `arbora/api/ops/finance.py` (module not yet created). Shape: `arbora/api/ops/finance.py` (new) declares `positions.*` and `orders.paper_submit` with `Executor.SERVICE`, `PrincipalRule.HUMAN_UNDELEGATED` and `SubjectRef`; it reuses `prepare_executor` and the `FORBIDDEN_OWNER` check in `arbora/api/invoke/steps.py:20`, and `arbora/finance/markets.py` for the venue binding. | Test: `tests/api/test_finance_served.py::test_paper_action_requires_account_ownership` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It asserts the owner succeeds once. It asserts another tenant gets `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED` and a controlled provider records zero calls. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018` | **Query, search, ontology and analytics operations (rollup).** Stage: served read operations over the caller's graph. Caller: MCP or HTTP → `invoke` → `EgMethod` binding → the hand-written medulla client under the caller's verified claims. Curated operations exist in `arbora/api/ops/query.py` (`query.uql`, `query.nl`), `query_engine.py` (`query.sparql`, `query.sql`, `ontology.inspect`, `ontology.schema.classes`, `ontology.validate`, `federation.sources.columns`) and `retrieval.py`. Every other engine method is served as the generated operation `eg.<service>.<Method>` (`load_eg_bindings`). Arbora adds no local ontology store and no local planner. Operations pass parameters as typed values; they never interpolate SQL text. Writes: none. Children: `.1` ontology and schema, `.2` query, search, indexes and series. Depends on: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_each_query_family_reaches_engine_with_tenant_and_budget` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.1` | **Sparql, schema and ontology operations.** Stage: served read operations. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/query_engine.py::operations` bindings. Rule: the supported set is `query.sparql` (`Method::Sparql`), `ontology.inspect` (`OntologyInspect`), `ontology.schema.classes` (`GraphSchemaClasses`), `ontology.schema.graph` (`GraphSchema`), `ontology.schema.list` (`GraphSchemaList`) and `ontology.validate` (`ShaclValidate`). Add `ontology.schema.graph` and `ontology.schema.list` to `query_engine.py`, each with scope `owl:read`. SQL table and column discovery for the caller's graph is `ontology.schema.list`. A foreign source's columns stay `federation.sources.columns`. Decision: the ontology operations without an engine method are not curated. Loading triples is `eg.<service>.AddTriples`. Catalogue listing is `eg.<service>.CatalogList`. Export is `query.sparql` with a CONSTRUCT query. Linting is `ontology.validate`. Generation belongs to the SDK ontology pack generator (`SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL`), not Arbora. Each operation takes the graph from the verified session, never from the request. Config: none. Writes: none. Depends on: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> engine call through the `EgMethod` bindings declared in `arbora/api/ops/query_engine.py:34::operations` (`query.sparql`, `ontology.inspect`, `ontology.schema.classes`, `ontology.validate`; `ontology.schema.graph` and `ontology.schema.list` are not yet declared). Shape: Add two `_read(...)` entries to `operations()` in `arbora/api/ops/query_engine.py` for `GraphSchema` and `GraphSchemaList` with scope `owl:read`, reusing the existing `_read` helper and the generated `EgSchemaRef` request and result paths; the graph comes from the verified session in the engine binding. | Test: `tests/api/test_query_ops.py::test_ontology_schema_graph_and_list_bind_engine_methods` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_sparql_and_schema_routes_reach_engine` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2` | **Uql, sql, search, index and series operations.** Stage: served read operations. Caller: `invoke` → `EgMethod` binding or the retrieval port. Rule: `query.uql` binds `Uql`. `query.sql` binds `Sql`. `query.nl` binds `NlQuery`. Search is `retrieval.search` (`ARBORA-OPS-R021.2.1`). Index operations are the generated `eg.<service>.SemanticIndex` and `SemanticSearch`. Series operations are the generated `eg.<service>.TsRange`, `TsWindow` and `TsListSeries`. These need no curated declaration. Every operation runs under the caller's tenant. Arbora forwards the caller budget unchanged (R018.2.2). Config: none. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2.2`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`. | Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_uql_sql_series_and_index_ops_reach_engine` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2.1` | **Distinct caller-bound query contracts.** `query.uql` binds EG `Uql` under `query:unified`, using the provider inner request-parameter schema. `query.nl` binds EG `NlQuery` under `query:nl`, accepts text only, and obtains its graph from the current verified caller session. Both preserve provider result and refusal contracts. Missing NL feature/planner refuses at the provider; there is no local planner, scope substitution, or automatic grant. Caller chain: `arbora/api/ops/query.py:18::operations` declares `query.uql` (`EgMethod Uql`) and `query.nl` (`EgMethod NlQuery`); reached through `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` or MCP `ask` via `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:317::dispatch_verb`. The `approval_ref` is a two-person approval receipt from the Arbora approval flow (`ARBORA-OPS-R863` pattern). The proposer cannot approve. Refusals: `PLAN_MISMATCH` for a changed repair, `POLICY_DENIED` for a missing approval, `UNAVAILABLE` for an absent runner. Writes: the repair activation in the engine and the audit reservation. Each connector server that declares ingest sources is one pack, with the server name as `pack_id`. A pack is in `error` when any of its sources is in `error`, else `active`. Without a composed runner the operation fails closed with UNAVAILABLE. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `arbora/ingest/service.py:302::list_packs` -> `arbora/ingest/connector_sources.py:437::ConnectorIngestRunner.list_packs`. Shape: `list_packs` reuses `_declarations` and `_record`, builds one `IngestPackRecord` per declaring server (`pack_id` is the server name) and marks `error` when any source record state is `error`; no runner raises `UNAVAILABLE` from `_bound_runner`. | `tests/ingest/test_connector_sources.py::test_packs_list_serves_one_pack_per_declaring_connector` lists the fixture pack as `active`, then as `error` after a failed sync, and refuses an unbound runner as UNAVAILABLE. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.8` | **A failed sync names its cause.** A failed source sync carries the SDK reason code and, for an engine refusal, the engine code. The sync receipt holds both, and the job's WorkItem stores them in `error_ref`. `ingest.jobs.status` reads them back. Arbora logs the source id, the reason, the engine code and the record count. No receipt, job or log line holds a record payload. A child result reaches arbora when one SSE event holds up to twice the delegated value bound. That bound is 4 MiB, so an event may hold 8 MiB. Validation run 2026-10-24 gap G13: two ServiceNow sources failed `tool_failed` with no stated reason. Each 50-record page exceeded the MCP client default event bound of 1 MiB. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:207` (`create_api_application`, route `/ops/{op_id}`) -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122::make_endpoint` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `ConnectorIngestRunner.sync_source` (`arbora/ingest/connector_sources.py:293`) -> `_run` (`:314`) -> `_error_ref` (`:59`); read back by `get_job_status` (`:572`) via `_parse_error_ref` (`:66`); child result bound in `arbora/fleet/multiplexer.py::_assert_bounded_delegated_value` (`:576`). Shape: `IngestSyncReceipt` (`arbora/ingest/service.py:28`) carries `reason` and `engine_code`; the failed WorkItem stores `ingest-source-error:<reason>[:<code>]` in `error_ref`; the child-result size bound is enforced in `arbora/fleet/multiplexer.py`. | `tests/ingest/test_connector_sources.py::test_a_failed_sync_carries_its_cause_to_the_receipt_and_job`; `::test_a_completed_sync_has_no_reason`. `tests/fleet/test_child_large_results.py::test_a_two_mib_child_result_reaches_arbora` reads a 2 MiB result from a real FastMCP child over HTTP. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.5.1` | **Current-plan read boundary.** Model: SDK-owned `CurrentPlanRequest`, `PlanItem`, `CurrentPlanSnapshot` and `CurrentPlanRead` from `agent_connector_sdk.current_plan` (SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL-R070). Arbora imports these values from a published SDK distribution that includes that contract. Each model refuses unknown fields and cannot change. A snapshot holds the run ID and a revision of zero or more. It also holds UTC observation and expiry times and ordered items. Each item has a status of `pending`, `in_progress` or `done`. An available empty plan differs from an unavailable read. Behavior: `runs.current_plan` reads the plan of one run through the injected `current_plan_reader` port. It passes the admitted execution context to the port; the verified caller supplies tenant and principal. Arbora stores no plan and does not rebuild a plan from stored messages. Wiring: `handle_runs_current_plan` uses `build_read_op` and the existing `kg:read` scope. `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py` lists `runs`. `runs.get` reuses `read_current_plan` for its `current_plan` field. Refusal: An absent port, an unknown run or a run of another caller returns the same unavailable read. It holds no items. A malformed result, a result for a different run, a future observation time or an expired snapshot also return unavailable. Parameters cannot supply caller authority. | `tests/api/test_runs_ops.py` covers caller forwarding, item order, the absent port, malformed parameters, malformed snapshots and each fail-closed projection. |

No plan store or producer admission is introduced. | `ARBORA-OPS-R844` | **Served memory read and write over EG KV.** Extends `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4`. `memory.read` maps to EG `KvGet` and `memory.write` to EG `KvPut`. A key is stored at `memory/<namespace>/<key>`: EG namespace `memory`, EG key `<namespace>/<key>`, default namespace `default`. Every result returns the entry version. An absent key reads as `found: false`. A compare-and-set mismatch returns `swapped: false` with the current version. Writes need an idempotency key, which EG replays. Scopes: `kv:read` and `kv:write`. Add field `content_origin: ContentOrigin = trusted` to `OpSpec` in `arbora/api/registry/spec.py`. The registry digest includes it. Mark `research.web` (ARBORA-OPS-R067) as `open_web`. Refusals: `INVALID_ARGUMENT` for an unknown value. Evidence: none. Writes: none. Depends on: none. | `ARBORA-OPS-R042.2` | **Default screen rules and output redactor.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Code: `arbora/api/invoke/screening.py` holds `default_screen_rules` and `default_output_redactor`, both registered by name in a `ScreenRegistry`. Rule: one default rule per attack class. Each matches hidden text (zero-width characters, CSS-hidden elements, HTML comments), rendered text instructions, or markup-embedded instructions aimed at that class. Findings in `memory`, `action`, `multi_agent` and `overseer` are `Fixed` quarantine classes. `perception` and `reasoning` findings are flagged and their spans removed. The redactor replaces these with `[REDACTED]`: bearer tokens, `sk-` style keys, cloud access keys and private-key blocks. It also masks values under keys named `password`, `secret` or `token`. Configuration: `content_screen.rules` and `content_screen.redactor` names in `~/.config/arbora/config.json`, defaults the two default names; `content_screen.max_scan_bytes` default 1048576, bounds `[4096, 16777216]`. Refusals: content above the scan limit is quarantined. Evidence: findings. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.1. Caller chain: `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021`) -> `_effect` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:258`) -> `screen_result` / `redact_result` in `arbora/api/invoke/screening.py` (to be written), selected by name from `ScreenRegistry`. Shape: Pure-function default rules and redactor in `arbora/api/invoke/screening.py`, registered by name; reuse the existing secret patterns of `arbora/security/log_redaction.py` where they match. | Test: `tests/api/test_content_screening.py::test_each_attack_class_and_masked_secret` (L1). It plants one case per class and one secret per pattern. |

For `memory.write` with `content_origin` set to `open_web` in its params, `invoke` calls `screen_params` before the effect. This row creates both call sites. | `ARBORA-OPS-R042.4` | **Screening refusals.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. ARBORA-OPS-R050.2.1 is retired into ARBORA-OPS-R071. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R071, MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R806. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_policy_plan_guard_served.py::test_policy_create_then_violating_plan_is_refused` (L3). |

Code: add to `arbora/api/ops/policy.py` the frozen Pydantic models `PolicyChange` (a `StrEnum`), `ConstitutionSource{repository, path, commit, blob_digest}` and `PolicyEntity{policy_ref, version_digest, constitution_ref, clause_digest, state}` with `state` one of `active`, `retired`. Add `constitution_ref` and `state` to `PolicyVersion` in `arbora/control_plane/policy/models.py`. Digests are SHA-256, 64 lowercase hex characters. Refusals: `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on validation failure. Evidence: none. Writes: none. Depends on: none. Caller chain: `operations()` in `arbora/api/ops/policy.py:47` (policy operations registered via `get_registry`, `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py:16`) -> new models in the same module; `PolicyVersion` is at `arbora/control_plane/policy/models.py:203`. Verified by the root slice test `tests/api/test_policy_plan_guard_served.py::test_policy_create_then_violating_plan_is_refused`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R050.2` | **Policy change behavior.** Stage: arbora served operations. Caller: `handle_policy_change` → `PolicyActivationPort.activate`. Code: `PolicyActivationPort` in `arbora/api/ops/policy.py`. Its engine adapter sends `Method::PolicyActivation` (MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R806) with the `ConstitutionSource`, the change kind and the review receipt digest produced by ARBORA-OPS-R071. Rule: arbora never reads clause text. It publishes the returned `PolicyEntity` references with `PolicyAuthority.publish` in `arbora/control_plane/policy/repository.py`. Refusals: see ARBORA-OPS-R053.4. Evidence: see ARBORA-OPS-R053.5. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.1, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R029. Caller chain: Planned: `arbora/api/http/app.py:208` -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122` (`make_endpoint`, `invoke` call at line 160) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`) -> `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py:313` -> `handle_retrieval_space` (new, beside `handle_retrieval_search` at `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py:76`) -> `build_space` (new, same file). MCP entry: `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:230` (`_invoke_and_render`, `projection.invoke` at line 244) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021`. Shape: `build_space(outcomes, plan)` is a pure function in `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py` over `RetrievalSearchOutcome` values (line 46) from the `Retriever.search` port; it unions refs in first-seen order and names `q01`, `q02`. K and the query cap are read from the decision, with the stated fallback only when it is absent. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_build.py::test_union_caps_and_directory_names` (L2). |

The handler calls `GovernedRetriever.retrieve` in `arbora/control_plane/retrieval/service.py`. No production constructor of `GovernedRetriever` exists. Add `_with_retriever` in `arbora/mcp_server/served_invocation.py`, called from `compose_served_invocation` in `arbora/mcp_server/served_invocation.py`. It builds the engine-backed retriever and binds `retriever`, which `retrieval.search` also uses. Refusals: `UNAVAILABLE` when no retriever is bound. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.2. Caller chain: Planned: `arbora/api/http/app.py:208` -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122` (`make_endpoint`, `invoke` call at line 160) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`) -> `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py:313` -> `handle_retrieval_space` (new) -> `bound_service(context, "retriever")` (`arbora/api/ops/_common.py:70`). No production code binds `retriever` today. Shape: Register a second `build_read_op` entry in `operations()` of `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py` (like `retrieval.search`, scope `memory:read`). `_with_retriever` follows the shape of `_with_ingest_runner`: return `bindings` unchanged unless composable, else `{**bindings, "retriever": ...}`. `GovernedRetriever.retrieve` (`arbora/control_plane/retrieval/service.py:61`) is synchronous over `RetrievalRequest`, so an async adapter implementing the `Retriever` port is needed. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_wiring.py::test_served_request_reaches_composed_retriever_and_missing_producer_refuses` (L3). |

Shape: Three guard checks at the top of `handle_retrieval_space` raising `OperationRefused` with `LOAD_CAP_EXCEEDED` (code in `arbora/api/errors.py:32`, public detail key `loaded_items` at line 294), `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and `UNAVAILABLE`, all before any `retriever` call; no new error plumbing. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_refusal.py::test_three_refusals_run_zero_queries` (L3). |

Tools/runner.worker -> McpToolSourceAdapter.extract -> generated SourceIngest. AU study_compare/admit consume EG verdicts; study_intake explicitly stores locator/digest and forbids copying the body. So it is not the missing selected-content ingestion producer. External engine, SDK, AU, Pipelines or WebUI behavior remains owned there; this row specifies only Arbora. | `ARBORA-OPS-R022.4` | **AU:53 Arbora definition slice.** AU:53 cross-owner slice. Residual: The contract does not define grading every output or converting checker findings into the next instruction. It also omits durable continuation until no fixes remain or a bound is reached. A once-per-evaluation-round gate plus a run API does not establish that feedback loop. Arbora minimum action: Extend the existing runner/evaluation loop contract with every-output checker feedback, findings-to-next-instruction. Durable continuation and no-fixes/bound stopping rules; preserve R022 fail-closed scope/idempotency criteria. Producer/consumer boundary: AU triggered runner/output -> checker/evaluation report -> EG authority and durable state. Arbora exposes the loop status/run/pause facade. The report-to-next-instruction and stopping links remain undefined. External engine, SDK, AU, Pipelines or WebUI behavior remains owned there; this row specifies only Arbora. Current IDs: `ARBORA-OPS-R022, MERISTEM-HARNESS-R021, MERISTEM-HARNESS-R003`. Source record SHA-256: `3432e0278c2f92fce06790822ed67bd032678dcf58ab47c2a76934dfd9356199`. | `ARBORA-OPS-R009.2` | **Refusal causes are logged.** The invoke pipeline and the error envelope must log the internal cause of an `UNAVAILABLE`, `INTERNAL` or `INDETERMINATE` outcome server-side. The log names the operation, the request ID and the exception. The client envelope stays redacted and carries the same request ID. A provider-schema refusal logs its first validation message, bounded to 300 characters. An engine refusal logs the engine code and its bounded diagnostic detail. Caller chain: `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:918` (`_dispatch_outcome`, warning at line 946 for `INTERNAL`, timeout warning at line 935) and `arbora/api/errors.py:490` (`to_envelope`, warning at line 503 for `UNAVAILABLE`/`INTERNAL`) ; reached from `arbora/api/http/app.py:208` -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122` (`make_endpoint`, `invoke` call at line 160) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`). Engine refusals log at `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py:296`; provider-schema refusals at `arbora/api/invoke/steps.py:160`. Shape: Reuses module-level `_logger` calls in `pipeline.py`, `errors.py`, `executor.py` and `steps.py`; the engine detail is cut to 300 characters and the provider-schema message to `_LOG_DETAIL_CHARS = 300` (`arbora/api/invoke/steps.py:17`). The client envelope is built only from reviewed fields, so no log text reaches it. | `tests/api/test_refusal_causes.py::test_refusal_cause_is_logged_with_request_id` observes the logged cause and the redacted envelope. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.4` | **A typed refusal names its reason, never its input.** An `INVALID_ARGUMENT` envelope keeps `field`, the field-name lists `fields`, `unexpected`, `missing`, `invalid` and `required`, and an Arbora-authored `reason`. Each value is bounded, and the envelope drops every other key. An input that misses its engine contract schema names its unexpected, missing and invalid fields, never a value. An engine refusal keeps its detail text in the operator log. The envelope gets only the first upper-snake reason code and its `at line:column` position. Arbora refuses a connector prompt call that omits a declared required argument. The `INVALID_ARGUMENT` refusal names the missing arguments, and arbora sends nothing to the child. A child that reports missing prompt arguments gets the same refusal. Any other child prompt failure is the child's own `CHILD_REFUSED` with its redacted detail, never `INTERNAL`. Validation run 2026-10-24 gaps G14 and G10: `query.uql` with `query` in place of `text` was refused with empty details. `create_branch_prompt` without arguments answered `INTERNAL`. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/app.py:208` -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:122` (`make_endpoint`, `invoke` call at line 160) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:985` (`_typed_refusal`) -> `arbora/api/errors.py:490` (`to_envelope`) -> `_public_details` (line 280) -> `_argument_details` (line 228); provider-schema path `arbora/api/invoke/steps.py:148` (`_validate_provider`). Prompt path: `arbora/fleet/gateway_ops.py:475` (`prompts_get`) -> `_require_prompt_arguments` (line 47, called at line 487) and `_prompt_refusal` (line 66, called at line 497). Shape: `_argument_details` in `arbora/api/errors.py` keeps only the reviewed list keys (`_ARGUMENT_LISTS`, line 215) and a bounded `reason`; `engine_reason_details` (line 249) keeps the upper-snake code and position. Prompt refusals raise `OperationRefused` or `FleetRefusal(code="CHILD_REFUSED")`, reusing the existing typed refusal path. | `tests/api/test_typed_refusal_details.py` drives the production registry through the invoke pipeline and the envelope. `tests/fleet/test_relay_session_recovery.py::test_a_prompt_called_without_its_arguments_is_a_typed_refusal` renders a real FastMCP child prompt; `::test_declared_required_prompt_arguments_are_checked_before_the_child`. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.6` | **Operator hook model and configuration.** Stage: invoke pipeline bootstrap. Caller: host bootstrap → `arbora/api/invoke/bootstrap.py` → `load_hooks` in new `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. The new module is reached from `InvokeServices` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`, which gains the field `hooks: HookChain`. Types: `HookPoint` (`input`, `output`), `HookSpec { name, point, op_ref }`, `HookDecision` (a closed union of `allow`, `redact { spans }` and `reject { code }`) and `Span { path, start, end }`. Configuration is the file `operator_hooks.json` under `config_dir()` of `arbora/core/settings.py`, key `hooks`: an ordered list of `HookSpec`. A hook is a reference to a registered operation. Arbora loads no code from the file. Bootstrap validation refuses a hook in three cases. Its `op_ref` is absent from the registry. Its operation has an effect other than `READ`. Its operation is another hook. A refusal stops the host start with the reason `hook_invalid`. Hooks run in declared order. Knob `operator_hooks.timeout_s` is `Fixed` at 2 seconds. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. Shape: one new file `hooks.py` holding the types, `load_hooks` and `HookChain`; validate through one table of rules, and reuse `Registry.get`. | `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7` | **Input hook runs before the input is admitted.** Stage: invoke pipeline, after `_resolve` and before `_confirmation_gate` in `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`). Caller: the served route → `invoke` → `HookChain.run_input` in `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. The chain calls each `input` hook with `{op_id, tenant, params}`. It invokes the hook operation through `invoke` itself with the host service principal, the flag `hooks_enabled` false and the same `request_id`. The hook sees the caller's tenant and never sees a credential. `allow` passes the params on. `redact` replaces each named span of a string param by the fixed text `[redacted]` and passes the result on. `reject` ends the call with `OpError(code)`, where `code` is the hook's code or `HOOK_REJECTED`. The next hook receives the output of the previous hook. A redacted param set must still pass `_arguments` validation. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.6`, `ARBORA-OPS-R042.3`. Shape: one method `run_input(request: HookRequest)` that dispatches through a table from decision kind to an apply function; reuse the span masker of `arbora/security/log_redaction.py`. Caller chain: Planned (not built): `arbora/api/http/routes.py:160` (`invoke`) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`; the hook call goes after `_resolve` at line 642 and before `_confirmation_gate` at line 744) -> new `HookChain.run_input` in `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_input_hook_allow_redact_and_reject` (served: HTTP and MCP through the registry `invoke`) asserts the three decisions and the order of two hooks. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.8` | **Output hook runs before a response leaves.** Stage: invoke pipeline, in `_execute` after `_dispatch_outcome` and before `_record` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`). Caller: the served route → `invoke` → `_execute` → `HookChain.run_output`. Each `output` hook receives `{op_id, tenant, result}`. The three decisions apply as in `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`. The outcome after the hooks is the outcome that `_record` writes to the effect journal. A replay therefore returns the same response. A `reject` at the output point replaces the result by `OpError(code)`. The effect has already happened, and the audit record states this with `effect_applied` true. An `OpError` result passes the output hook only when the hook has the flag `include_errors`. The default is false. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`, `ARBORA-OPS-R042.3`. Shape: reuse the apply table of `run_input`; add the call as one line in `_execute`. Caller chain: Planned (not built): `arbora/api/http/routes.py:160` (`invoke`) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`) -> `_execute` (line 998) -> after `_dispatch_outcome` (line 918) and before `_record` (line 589) -> new `HookChain.run_output` in `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_output_hook_redacts_and_rejects_and_replay_matches` (served: HTTP and MCP) asserts the replayed response equals the hooked response. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.9` | **Hooks fail closed and record their decisions.** Stage: invoke pipeline. A hook that raises, returns an unknown decision or exceeds `operator_hooks.timeout_s` (`Fixed`, 2 seconds) rejects the call. The code is `HOOK_FAILED`. The envelope names the hook and holds no hook output. No later hook runs. The effect journal receives the rejection like any other outcome. `audit_event` in `arbora/api/invoke/audit.py` gains the field `hooks`, a list of `{name, point, decision, duration_ms}`. The list holds no matched text. The same list enters the run trace when the call belongs to a run: `arbora/api/ops/run_graph.py` reads it from the audit event. Add `HOOK_FAILED` and `HOOK_REJECTED` to `arbora/api/errors.py`. Writes: the audit event only. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009.8`. Shape: one `asyncio.wait_for` wrapper in `hooks.py`; reuse the `_typed_refusal` path for the envelope. Caller chain: Planned (not built): `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` (`invoke`) -> new `HookChain` in `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`; the rejection is recorded by `_record` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:589`) and `audit_event` (`arbora/api/invoke/audit.py:13`) gains `hooks`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_timeout_error_and_unknown_decision_reject_with_hook_failed` (served) and `::test_decisions_are_recorded_without_matched_text` (served). | `ARBORA-OPS-R700` | **Readiness snapshot joins independent probes.** `arbora/observability/readiness.py` exposes `collect_readiness_snapshot(probes, required, deadline_s, now)` returning the `arbora.readiness.v1` envelope. Probes run concurrently on a pool sized to the probe count; a probe that raises, overruns its deadline or returns an unknown state reports `unavailable`, never `ready`. A failed or missing required check (`engine`, `catalog`, `synthetic_query` by default) makes the snapshot `unavailable`; any other non-ready check makes it `degraded`. Identity is `ready` only for a session whose actor is authenticated. Every call re-probes; nothing is cached. `GET /dashboard/readiness` serves the snapshot from the engine probe and the probes added with `register_probe`. Budget: rollup under 1 ms for 16 probes, memory under 2 MiB beyond probe cost; wall time is bounded by the deadline. Caller chain: HTTP `GET /dashboard/readiness` (`arbora/gateway/dashboard_api.py:226`, `readiness_check`) -> `arbora/observability/readiness.py::collect_readiness_snapshot` (line 74). | `ARBORA-OPS-R848.1` | **Source reads.** Serve `source.tree`, `source.blob`, `source.history` and `source.blame` as one typed read-only tool each. Each reads the engine's branch-aware source projection: one repository's `Branch` node, its live `FileVersion` memberships and their `Blob` content. Admission is per principal, with the exact engine scopes the read uses. Tree, history and blame use `node:read` and `edge:read`. Blob adds `blob:read`. Path admission uses the engine's row-level visibility under the caller's own claims. The stateless native reader composes at the serving root. An uncomposed reader fails closed with a typed `UNAVAILABLE`. The reader serves only what the substrate stores. Directories derive from the `FileVersion` path prefixes. The projection stores no directory nodes. `source.blame` reports the membership edge's per-file last touch. Its `line_level` is always `false`. `source.history` lists the last touch per live ref, without a commit DAG. Proving test: `tests/api/test_source_ops.py::test_source_reads_served` with `tests/mcp_server/test_source_reader.py`. Caller: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `source.land` → new `arbora/api/ops/source_landing.py::land_changeset`. Step 1: the handler materializes the changeset tree through the engine `Tree` read. Step 2: the `GateRunner` port runs the fast gates against that tree: format, hygiene, secrets, types and tests-fast. The default runner calls the repository-manager connector's gate tool through `fleet.call`. Each gate verdict writes to the engine as a `GateVerdict` record (`MEDULLA-SCM-R008`). Step 3: if all verdicts pass, the handler asks the engine to accept the changeset. A failing gate refuses `POLICY_DENIED` with reason `fast_gate_failed` and the gate id. Nothing lands. Step 4: after landing, the full suite runs asynchronously through the same port. On failure the engine records the bisect and revert decision (`MEDULLA-SCM-R009`). arbora keeps no merge queue and decides no revert. Config: `source.land.fast_gate_timeout_s` in `~/.config/arbora/config.yaml`, default 300, range 30 to 3600. A timeout counts as a failed gate. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R848.2`, `MEDULLA-SCM-R008`, `MEDULLA-SCM-R009`. Params: `identity` and `principal_ref`. The handler generates a 32-byte random password. It stores only the salted hash in the engine through the wire-login method of `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R007`. It writes the cleartext to the platform secret store under `wire_access/<identity>`. It returns the secret reference and never the password. `wire_access.revoke` marks the identity revoked in the engine and deletes the secret. Revocation takes effect for new logins within 60 s. `wire_access.grant` calls `eg.security.WireLoginGrant`. `wire_access.revoke` calls `eg.security.WireLoginRevoke`. The credential verifier goes to the engine. The secret goes to the platform secret store. Depends on: `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R007`. After approval, execution commits through `eg.coordination.DecisionLog` `learn` `MovePointer`, citing the committed `Promote` record. `revision.rollback` pins `expected_champion_ref` and calls `DecisionLog` `learn` `MovePointer` to the parent in the pointer stack. Promote or rollback without an approved plan refuses `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`. A vetoed plan refuses `POLICY_DENIED` with reason `approval_vetoed`. A caller without `revision:admin` refuses `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. Engine refusals pass through with their own codes: `PROMOTION_UNEVALUATED`, `PROMOTION_NO_BASELINE`, `PROMOTION_EVAL_TOO_SMALL`, `EVAL_SET_NOT_INDEPENDENT`, `PROMOTION_CAS_CONFLICT`, `PROMOTION_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`, `ROLLBACK_NO_PARENT` and the `PROGRAM_*` job codes. Every refusal leaves the pointer unchanged and makes zero `MovePointer` calls. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R860.2`, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R805.4`, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R810.4`. Caller chain: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/http/routes.py:160` over HTTP, `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:244` over MCP) -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `arbora/api/ops/program.py::handle_revision_promote` (new), plan gate at `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:744::_confirmation_gate`. ## Shared execution explorer (specified)

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.8` | **Project an admitted execution graph.** Stage: served read operation `runs.execution_graph`. Caller: `invoke` → `handle_runs_execution_graph` → `NativeRunReader.execution_graph` → `arbora/api/ops/run_graph.py::project_graph`. Rule: keep replay semantics and decoder invariants. A new projection is versioned (`SCHEMA` in `run_replay.py`); an unsupported version refuses and never reinterprets event task ids. Return a bounded snapshot and ordered updates from the same owner-checked plan and `RunEvent` rows. Keep the root work item, the run and the run-local step as distinct identities. Node keys are `step_key(run_id, task_id)`. Edges are `spawned_child` or `depends_on`, never both implied. Dependencies never imply parentage. Only producer-declared lineage appears. The snapshot binds root, schema version, covered event cursor and completeness. Updates keep native event ids and sequence. Config: `runs_graph_max_nodes` default 500 and `runs_graph_max_edges` default 2000 in `~/.config/arbora/config.yml`, ranges [10, 5000] and [10, 20000]. Past a bound the snapshot is `partial`. Refusals: a gap, a conflicting identity or missing lineage returns an explicit `unavailable` or `partial` state. Arbora invents no edge. Writes: none. Depends on: R021.4.1, R021.4.2. | Test: `tests/api/test_execution_graph.py::test_snapshot_plus_updates_equals_fresh_snapshot` (L1) asserts equality at one cursor. Test: `tests/api/test_runs_served.py::test_fan_out_fan_in_reconnect_and_foreign_owner` (L3) covers repeated step names, bounded pages and foreign-owner refusal before any payload read. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.9` | **Project permitted node records and capabilities.** Stage: served read operation `runs.node` beside `runs.execution_graph`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/runs.py::handle_runs_node` (new) → `NativeRunReader.node`. Rule: authorize each selected node through its explicit owner binding before any transcript or execution read. Keep public summaries, model text, tool records and transcript as distinct fields, each with provenance, visibility, redaction and availability. Return four capabilities: `can_read_events`, `can_read_transcript`, `can_message_active` and `can_continue_completed`. Each is a boolean with a typed unavailable reason; unknown defaults to unavailable. Only an owner-issued target binding enables interaction. A trace id, step id, span name or readable event never grants chat authority. Writes recheck authority. Refusals: a forged node-owner pair returns `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. Private reasoning and secrets never appear. An absent transcript differs from an empty one. Writes: none. Depends on: R021.4.8. Caller chain: `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021`) -> `arbora/api/ops/runs.py::handle_runs_node` (new, beside `handle_runs_execution_graph` at line 129; `operations` at line 142) -> `arbora/mcp_server/run_reader.py::NativeRunReader.node` (new, beside `execution_graph` at line 112). Shape: Add a `node` method to `NativeRunReader` that calls `_owned` (line 58) first, then a `runs.node` `OpSpec` in `arbora/api/ops/runs.py` built like `runs.execution_graph`; capability fields default to unavailable. | Test: `tests/api/test_runs_ops.py::test_node_capabilities_default_unavailable` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_runs_served.py::test_node_records_owner_scoped_on_both_transports` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It rejects forged pairs and keeps interaction disabled for a flat replay. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.8.1` | **Negotiated flat execution snapshot.** Serve `runs.execution_graph` with required `schema_version=arbora-execution-graph-v1`, preserving the default `runs.get` and `runs.events` payloads and replay v1 decoder. Reuse canonical root WorkItem/run admission before native current-plan or event reads. Project only declared task IDs, dependencies and plan digest, with stable run-qualified step keys and predecessor-to-dependent edges. Recheck the plan binding after collecting events. Use a bounded, contiguous ordered event prefix for evidenced lifecycle only; absent step evidence remains unobserved. Separate complete declared topology from explicitly incomplete lifecycle. No prompt, output, conversation, spawn edge or interaction authority is projected. Unsupported schemas, unavailable or expired plans, changed plan bindings, malformed histories, and histories beyond the census bound fail closed. Caller chain: Over HTTP `arbora/api/http/app.py:205` route loop -> `arbora/api/http/routes.py:160`, over MCP `arbora/api/mcp/verbs.py:244`, both -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021::invoke` -> `arbora/api/ops/runs.py:129::handle_runs_execution_graph` -> `arbora/mcp_server/run_reader.py:112::NativeRunReader.execution_graph`. Shape: `NativeRunReader.execution_graph` checks the owner with `_owned`, reads the native snapshot twice around a bounded `_page` collection, and projects with `arbora/api/ops/run_graph.py:143::project_graph` (`declared_topology` at line 103, `unavailable_graph` at line 84). | Pure and served-source tests in `tests/api/test_execution_graph.py` cover fan-out/fan-in, repeated local IDs across runs, private-field omission, exact root/run/plan binding, owner-first refusal, immutable replay v1 behavior and event/byte bounds. Registry HTTP and MCP invoke the same native reader seam. These source tests do not establish installed native-service or nested execution acceptance; R021.4.8 and R021.4.9 remain open. Test: `tests/api/test_execution_graph.py::test_http_and_mcp_share_native_owner_checked_projection`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.8.2` | **Nested lineage and snapshot reconciliation.** Stage: served read operation `runs.execution_graph`, nested slice. Caller: as R021.4.8, with a producer that declares child runs. Producer: the harness emits `spawned_child` events with `parent_run_id` and `child_run_id` (`MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R060`). Medulla stores them as `RunEvent` facts (`MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R009.100`). Rule: serve only producer-declared child and grandchild identities, each with its own authorized run binding. Distinguish `spawned_child` edges from `depends_on` edges. Reconcile a snapshot plus ordered updates to a fresh snapshot at the same cursor. Keep event identity across reconnect. Bound each page, run census, node set and depth with `runs_graph_max_depth` default 4, range [1, 8]. After truncation the snapshot says `partial`. A flat plan step never supplies a child conversation or interaction target. Refusals: conflicting duplicates, gaps, cross-run payloads and missing lineage return typed states. Writes: none. Depends on: R021.4.8, `MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R060`. Caller chain: As R021.4.8: `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021`) -> `arbora/api/ops/runs.py:129::handle_runs_execution_graph` -> `arbora/mcp_server/run_reader.py:112::NativeRunReader.execution_graph`, extended to follow `spawned_child` events. Shape: Extend `arbora/api/ops/run_graph.py` (`ExecutionGraphRead.supports_spawned_children` is `Literal[False]` at line 63 today) with child edges and the `runs_graph_max_depth` bound, and make `NativeRunReader` authorize each child run through `_owned`. | Test: `tests/api/test_runs_served.py::test_root_child_grandchild_over_both_transports` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine with a recorded producer trace. It covers restart, reconnect, duplicate and gap handling and foreign-owner refusal. Source tests of R021.4.8.1 do not satisfy this row. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R024.6` | **Derivation status read.** Stage: served read. Operation `participant.derivation.status` (verb `ask`, effect `READ`, scope `participant:read`) in new `arbora/api/ops/participants.py`. | `ARBORA-OPS-R061.4` | **Usage index refusals and latency profile.** Stage: arbora served operations. Rule: authorize and bind the tenant before engine dispatch. Absent, invalid or exceeded latency evidence returns `UNAVAILABLE` with no aggregates. Configuration: key `usage_index.p95_bound_ms` in `~/.config/arbora/config.json`, default 500, bounds `[50, 5000]`. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R061.2. | `ARBORA-OPS-R062.4` | **Revocation, refusals and visibility limit.** Stage: arbora gateway stream. Rule: on session revocation the route stops delivery within one poll interval and closes with code 4401. The client clears protected state. A stale incarnation returns `STALE_CURSOR`. Missing scope returns `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. The commit-to-visible limit is configuration `graph_changes.commit_to_visible_p95_ms`, default 2000, bounds `[100, 60000]`. The browser measures it over workload W-chg. W-chg is 50 committed changes per second for 60 seconds, 16 subscribers and a 10000-node graph. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R062.2. Caller chain: The route `graph_changes_websocket` in `arbora/gateway/graph_changes_ws.py`, mounted next to `register_dashboard_routes` (`arbora/gateway/dashboard_api.py:402`); cursor refusals come from `arbora/api/ops/graph_changes.py::handle_graph_changes_subscribe` (not yet built). Shape: Poll loop in the new websocket module modelled on `_stream_dashboard` (`arbora/gateway/dashboard_api.py`), re-checking the session each interval and closing with code 4401; limits are config keys read once at route setup. | Test: `tests/api/test_graph_changes_served.py::test_revocation_stops_delivery_and_limit_is_measured` (L3). |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R071` | **Review-receipt producer for reviewed repository policy.** Subsystem: approvals. Companion of `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.7` and `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.8`. Operation `policy.review.approve` in new `arbora/api/ops/policy_review.py`, registered in the curated module loop of `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py::get_registry`. Caller: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `policy_review.py::handle_policy_review_approve`. `Confirm.PLAN`, `Idempotency.KEY_REQUIRED`, `AuditClass.IDENTITY_CHAIN`, scope `policy:review`. The receipt binds nine fields: repository, path, commit, blob digest, candidate-set digest and invariant generation. It also binds the expected active-policy revision, the reviewing actor and the receipt digest (SHA-256, 64 lowercase hex). The handler reads the current candidate set and invariant generation from the engine. A stale value refuses `PLAN_STALE`. Otherwise it returns the receipt. A rejected review returns no receipt. A source-file change uses governed write-back plus approval (`arbora/api/ops/write_back.py`). arbora holds no policy, grammar or activation authority. The engine validates the receipt. Depends on: `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.7`, `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R012.8`. Caller chain: `arbora/api/http/routes.py:160` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py:1021` -> `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke` -> `arbora/api/ops/policy_review.py::handle_policy_review_approve` (new; `arbora/api/ops/policy.py` exists as a neighbour module). | `ARBORA-OPS-R072` | **Paper-shortfall maintenance operation.** Subsystem: maintenance operations. Companion of `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.1`. Caller: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `maintenance.py::handle_paper_shortfall_run`. `Executor.SERVICE`, `Effect.DESTRUCTIVE`, `Confirm.PLAN`, `Idempotency.KEY_REQUIRED`, `AuditClass.IDENTITY_CHAIN`. Params: `owner_authorization_ref`, `expected_revision`, `cost_ceiling` (integer cents, 0 to 1,000,000) and `admission_verdict_ref`. The handler refuses before any effect in three cases. A missing authorization refuses `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. A stale revision refuses `PLAN_STALE`. A cost above the ceiling refuses `POLICY_DENIED` with reason `cost_over_ceiling`. It calls the engine maintenance method named in `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.1` and returns the result receipt the engine reads back. It purges nothing that is leased, authoritative or the last source copy. The engine enforces this and arbora passes its refusal through. The dispatch target is the scholarx tool `paper_maintenance_apply` (`CONN-SCHOLARX-API-COVERAGE-R002`). The request carries the `PaperMaintenanceOrder` fields defined there. Depends on: `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.1`, `CONN-SCHOLARX-API-COVERAGE-R002`. Shape: New `arbora/api/ops/maintenance.py` with an `operations()` tuple of `OpSpec` (registered in the curated module loop of `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py:16` (`get_registry`)), params as `Params` subclasses from `arbora/api/ops/_common.py`; `Effect.DESTRUCTIVE` with `Confirm.PLAN`; pre-effect checks (`SCOPE_REQUIRED`, `PLAN_STALE`, `POLICY_DENIED` reason `cost_over_ceiling`) run before the single engine call, and the engine refusal is passed through. | Test: `tests/api/test_maintenance_served.py::test_shortfall_receipt_and_zero_effect_refusals` (served). It asserts a receipt on success and zero engine effects on each refusal. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R073` | **Host resource-grant binding for incremental indexing.** Subsystem: admission. Companion of `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.4`. The ledger keys grants by caller tenant. The grant limit is the config key `resource_grants.max_working_bytes` in `~/.config/arbora/config.yaml` (default 8,589,934,592 bytes, range 1,048,576 to 1,099,511,627,776). `admit` records the positive `max_working_bytes` of the job at admission. An over-grant request refuses `LOAD_CAP_EXCEEDED` before the job is admitted. `release` runs on success, cancellation and failure, including a failed admission. The engine keeps allocation accounting. Depends on: `MEDULLA-REPO-INGEST-R006.4`. Shape: New `arbora/api/ops/_grants.py` holds a per-tenant ledger with `admit` and `release` (released in a `finally`), limit read from config key `resource_grants.max_working_bytes`; over-grant raises `OpError("LOAD_CAP_EXCEEDED")` before the job is admitted. | Test: `tests/api/test_ingest_grants_served.py::test_grant_recorded_refused_and_released` (served). It asserts baseline return after success, cancel and failure. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R863` | **Issue a recommendation approval receipt bound to a record digest.** Subsystem: approvals. Caller chain: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `arbora/api/ops/decide.py::handle_recommendation_approve`. Operation `decide.recommendation.approve`: `PrincipalRule.HUMAN`, `Idempotency.KEY_REQUIRED`, `AuditClass.IDENTITY_CHAIN`, scope `recommendation:approve`. Params: `record_id` and `record_digest` (SHA-256, 64 lowercase hex). The handler checks the scope first and makes no engine call on failure. The reviewer is the verified caller. It passes the receipt to the service-executed `decide.commit` (`ARBORA-OPS-R021.1`), which sends it to `DecisionCommit` or `DecisionLog` `Commit`. A digest mismatch refuses `PLAN_MISMATCH`. Depends on: `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R019.100.2`, `ARBORA-OPS-R021.1`. `decide.commit` (`ARBORA-OPS-R021.1`) is the caller that passes the receipt to `DecisionCommit`, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R019.100.1`. The evidence streams are those of `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R019.100.1`. Register it only when the pinned engine contract publishes the named method. Until then `get_registry` omits it. A registry test asserts the absence. Shape: `handle_recommendation_approve` in `arbora/api/ops/decide.py` reuses the scope check and the idempotency store of the invoke pipeline; no new receipt store. | Test: `tests/api/test_decide_ops.py::test_approval_commits_once_and_scope_refusal_precedes_engine` (served). A reviewer outside scope makes zero engine calls. |

Shape: Extends `RetrievalSearchParams` in `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py` with `decision_record_id`; the handler resolves the bound provider from the composed retriever registry (service bound via `bound_service` in `arbora/api/ops/_common.py`) and compares config digest before calling `search`. | Test: `tests/api/test_retrieval_ops.py::test_bound_provider_only_and_zero_alternate_calls_on_refusal` (served). Shape: New `arbora/api/invoke/autonomy.py` called next to the confirm checks in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`; it queries the engine verdict and, on `RequireConfirmation`, reuses the approved-plan lookup of `arbora/api/invoke/plan.py` (`PlanStore.validate`/`consume`); fails closed with `UNAVAILABLE`. | Test: `tests/test_autonomy_confirmation.py::test_irreversible_action_waits_for_approval` (served). A disabled or denied provider refuses before any medulla call. Retry and attempt accounting live here, and one invocation needs one receipt. Depends on: MEDULLA-MEDIA-VOCAB-R001.101.4, MEDULLA-MEDIA-VOCAB-R001.101.5. The signature check uses the shared verifier of `SCION-SCM-R003.2` and the secret reference of the connector. A bad signature refuses with `SCM_SIGNATURE_INVALID` and writes nothing. A polling fallback runs per followed proposal when no webhook arrived for `development.scm_poll_interval_s` (starts at 120 s). It calls the connector list tools with a stored cursor. Arbora stores only the cursor. Caller chain: HTTP route in `arbora/gateway/graph_api.py` → new `arbora/development/scm_intake.py::handle_scm_event` → `fleet.call` normalizer → engine `DeliveryFeedback`. Shape: New `scm_intake.py` that reuses the signed-ingress pattern of `arbora/messaging/alert_intake.py` and the fleet client of `arbora/api/ops/fleet.py`. Depends on: `ARBORA-MESSAGING-R008.1`, `SCION-SCM-R003.2`, `MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.5`. | Test: `tests/development/test_scm_intake_served.py::test_signed_event_reaches_delivery_feedback_and_bad_signature_refuses` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R896.9` | **Feedback admission stamps the acting principal.** Before arbora forwards a person's event, it maps the code-host actor to an arbora principal. The mapping reads the identity links of the access registry. The event carries `admitted_principal` only when that principal holds `source:write` on the repository. Otherwise the event goes on as context with no principal. A bot, an automated reviewer or a check event never carries a principal. Quoted text in an event never changes a scope or a policy. The engine uses the stamp for mandate revalidation (`MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.6`). Caller chain: `handle_scm_event` (`ARBORA-OPS-R896.8`) → new `arbora/development/scm_intake.py::admit_actor` → `arbora/security/access.py` scope check. Shape: One function `admit_actor(event, repository) -> str | None` in `scm_intake.py` that reuses the existing scope check; no new identity store. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R896.8`. | Test: `tests/development/test_scm_intake_served.py::test_only_principals_with_source_write_are_stamped` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R896.10` | **Merge arming runs only on an owner approval of the head commit.** `development.delivery.arm` (verb `act`, scope `source:write`) asks `Decide` with `MergeArming` (`MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.10`). On `arm` it calls the connector arm tool with the expected head commit and the approval reference. The arm tools belong to the code-host connector rows that `MEDULLA-SDLC-R019` lists. On `disarm` it calls the matching disarm tool. On `hold` it calls nothing. The approval is a receipt of `ARBORA-OPS-R863` bound to the head commit digest and issued to a human owner principal. The registry denies every merge tool and every arm tool to the platform principal without that receipt. Refusals: `MERGE_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `MERGE_HEAD_MISMATCH`, `MERGE_BY_PLATFORM_REFUSED`. Arbora never merges a proposal itself. Caller chain: `invoke` → new `arbora/api/ops/development.py::handle_delivery_arm` → engine `Decide` → `fleet.call` arm tool. Shape: One handler beside `handle_run_start` that reuses the receipt check of `handle_recommendation_approve` in `arbora/api/ops/decide.py`. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R863`, `ARBORA-OPS-R896.7`, `MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.10`. | Test: `tests/api/test_delivery_ops_served.py::test_arm_needs_owner_receipt_on_head_and_platform_never_merges` (L3: HTTP and MCP). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R896.11` | **Delivery writes to the code host are governed effects.** Every outside write of a delivery goes through the governed write-back path. The writes are push, open, comment, thread reply, thread resolve, close, arm and disarm. Each write carries an idempotency key of delivery ID, assignment ID and step. Each write records a receipt. After a restart an effect without a receipt is `uncertain`. Arbora reconciles it with a read before any retry (`SCION-GOVERNED-WRITEBACK-R004.11`, `SCION-GOVERNED-WRITEBACK-R004.14`). A retry of an uncertain effect without reconciliation refuses with `EFFECT_UNCERTAIN`. Caller chain: meristem host fleet port → `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/write_back.py::preview_write_back` → governed apply → `fleet.call`. Shape: Extend `preview_write_back` with the delivery idempotency key and reuse the existing receipt reservation; no second effect log. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R896.7`, `SCION-GOVERNED-WRITEBACK-R004.11`. | Test: `tests/api/test_delivery_ops_served.py::test_delivery_writes_are_idempotent_and_uncertain_effects_reconcile` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R896.12` | **A delivery assignment starts on a reserved development lane.** For each pending assignment of `MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.3`, arbora reserves a development lane through the engine lane methods. It then starts one meristem run with the delivery context of `MERISTEM-SCM-R012.1`. It delivers new inputs at the next step boundary through the host input port. The lane quota caps parallel assignments at `development.delivery.max_parallel` (starts at 4). One extra slot stays for a person's input, so new work never blocks a person. A run that loses its lane lease ends its assignment, and the engine recovers it (`MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.13`). A start without a free slot queues and refuses nothing. Caller chain: `handle_run_start` (`ARBORA-OPS-R896.3`) → new `arbora/development/assignments.py::start_assignment` → `arbora/fleet/scaling/development_lane.py::EngineNativeDevelopmentLaneTransport.reserve` → meristem run start. Shape: New `assignments.py` that reuses `EngineNativeDevelopmentLaneTransport` and the run start path of `arbora/api/ops/runs.py`. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R896.3`, `MEDULLA-SDLC-R019.3`, `MERISTEM-SCM-R012.1`. | Test: `tests/development/test_assignments_served.py::test_assignments_reserve_lanes_and_keep_a_person_slot` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R896.13` | **Hot-path proof: a delivery runs from slice to merge over MCP.** Caller chain: MCP `act` `development.run.start` → `handle_run_start` → `start_assignment` → recorded meristem run → `handle_scm_event` → `handle_delivery_arm`. The L4 journey uses a loopback engine and recorded code-host events. It starts a run, and the engine opens a delivery. A failed check event reaches the delivery. A pushed revision gives `disarm`. An owner receipt on the new head gives `arm`, and arbora calls the arm tool once. A merge event ends the delivery. The journey asserts zero merge calls by the platform principal. Shape: New `tests/api/test_delivery_surface_served.py` that reuses the fixtures of `tests/api/test_development_surface_served.py`. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R896.7` to `ARBORA-OPS-R896.12`. | Test: `tests/api/test_delivery_surface_served.py::test_delivery_runs_from_slice_to_merge_over_mcp` (L4). |
